# M6 App Shell, Home, Updater and Onboarding Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Turn the capture-and-edit core into the finished menu bar app. Only one copy runs at a time; Stickermine launches at login (on by default); a launch from Finder, Spotlight or `open` opens the Home window, while a login launch opens nothing unless an update turns up about 6 s later. Home shows every sticker ever made (newest first; a click places it again, a right-click deletes it from history after saying how many placements go with it), the settings (the capture-shortcut recorder with the macOS-shortcut warning, the menu bar icon, launch at login, hiding stickers from screen sharing, the Screen Recording state with a way to fix it), an About popover, Quit and, while there is an update, the update strip. The self-updater is Pullcord's. The first launch places three tutorial stickers; the fifth placement of the user's own stickers brings up a one-time support ask. Quitting, terminating and updating all run the same cleanup. A library saved by a newer Stickermine is explained in Home. The app gets its icon.

**Architecture:** `AppDelegate` stays the owner of every controller. New pure pieces carry the rules and are tested without windows: `Settings` (the UserDefaults keys, their registered defaults and typed accessors, `{k, m}` shortcut storage), `SystemShortcuts` (matching a chord against the `CopySymbolicHotKeys` table, injected in tests), `ShortcutRecording.outcome` (what one key press does to the recorder), `SingleInstance.incumbent` and `isLoginLaunch` (which copy stays, and whether the launch Apple event says "login item"), `LaunchPlan.decide` (what a launch opens), `Updater`'s version compare, release parsing, Homebrew detection and helper script, `HistoryList` (which stickers the grid shows, and the delete confirmation's words), `TutorialLayout` (where the tutorials go), `SupportAskRule` and `SupportAsk.frame`, and the words Home shows (`SettingsText`, `AboutLinks`, `VersionCheck`, `UpdateStrip.content`). Home is an AppKit window (`HomeWindowController`, `HomeWindow`) hosting SwiftUI (`HomeView`, `HistoryGrid`, `SettingsSection`, `AboutPopover`, `UpdateStrip`) driven by `HomeModel`, an `ObservableObject` that writes the settings and asks the app to act through `HomeModel.Actions` closures, so tests drive it with a fake login-item service, a fake Screen Recording check and recorded actions. `ShortcutRecorderButton` is an AppKit button (a local key monitor, Pullcord's `RecorderButton` rules) wrapped for SwiftUI. `LoginItem` wraps `SMAppService.mainApp` behind a protocol. `TutorialStickers` draws its three masters in code (`TutorialArt`), saves them like any sticker under `LibraryStore.tutorialIDs` and places them; `StickerManager.masterChanged(for:)` re-renders tutorial 1 after a shortcut change. `SupportAsk` is a non-activating panel that declines itself on any click elsewhere. Delete from History is `HistoryDeletion.delete` (cancel the session working on that sticker, then `StickerManager.deleteSticker`).

**Tech Stack:** Swift 5 language mode, AppKit (`NSWindow`, `NSPanel`, `NSStatusItem`, local and global event monitors, Apple events), SwiftUI in `NSHostingView`, ServiceManagement (`SMAppService`), Carbon (`CopySymbolicHotKeys`, key codes, Apple event constants), ImageIO (`CGImageSourceCreateThumbnailAtIndex`), Core Graphics with AppKit text drawing (the tutorial masters), `URLSession` (the update check), Swift Testing, `xcodebuild` (Xcode 27 beta locally, Xcode 26.6 on CI), `qlmanage` and `sips` (the icon script).

**Spec:** `design/specs/2026-10-12-stickermine-v1-design.md`. This milestone implements section 13 apart from the status item basics M4 built (the menu's Open Stickermine… and hiding the icon; Lifecycle: single instance, launch at login, what each launch does, reopen; the Home window: history grid, settings section, footer with the About popover and Quit, update strip; the Updater; First run; Support ask), the parts of section 12 M2 left for it (the Settings keys and defaults, including registering the hidden `clickThroughFallback`; Delete from History with its confirmation, cancelling a session for that sticker, and render completions finding their placement gone; the history thumbnails; Home's notice for a library saved by a newer build), section 5.10's termination cleanup on every path (applicationWillTerminate, the menu's and Home's Quit, before the updater's helper), section 14's Screen Recording state in Home (re-checked when Home becomes key, with the permission panel and the relaunch fallback behind it), section 16's app icon (`design/make-icon.sh`, the committed `AppIcon.appiconset` on the 824/1024 grid), and section 18's shortcut-storage and system-shortcut tests. The plan index with the Interface Contract is `design/plans/2026-10-12-stickermine-v1-plan.md`; the M1–M5 plans are `design/plans/2026-10-12-m1-foundation.md`, `design/plans/2026-10-12-m2-model-displays.md`, `design/plans/2026-10-12-m3-styles.md`, `design/plans/2026-10-12-m4-capture-picker.md` and `design/plans/2026-10-12-m5-editing.md`.

## Contract changes

Signature changes (every call written against the index still compiles):

1. **The spike's click-through decision moves into `Settings.clickThroughFallbackByDefault`, and `AppDelegate.clickThroughFallbackByDefault` becomes `nonisolated static let clickThroughFallbackByDefault = Settings.clickThroughFallbackByDefault`** (the contract name stays). `Settings.swift` is pure (spec section 4: no AppKit; it is on `LibraryModelTests.pureLogicFilesStayFreeOfAppKit`'s list), so it must not read the `@MainActor` `NSApplicationDelegate`; `Settings.register(in:)` and the nonisolated `UserDefaults.clickThroughFallback` getter read `Settings.clickThroughFallbackByDefault` instead. Its value is the one M2 Task 12 Step 5 chose from the spike (`false` unless the fallback became the default): Task 1 Step 3 copies it from the current `AppDelegate.swift` before Step 5 rewrites that line, and Task 14's complete `AppDelegate.swift` keeps reading it from `Settings`, so a `true` decision is never reverted.
2. **The contract's `enum Settings` shadows SwiftUI's `Settings` scene inside the module.** `StickermineApp` therefore writes `SwiftUI.Settings { EmptyView() }`. The name stays as the index has it.
3. **`StatusItemController.makeMenu(shortcut:)` returns `(menu:, newSticker:, openHome:, quit:)`**: M4's three-element tuple gains the Open Stickermine… item. Its only callers are `StatusItemController.init` and `StatusItemControllerTests`.

Additions (Task 17 copies them into the index):

- `App/Settings.swift`: `enum Settings { static let clickThroughFallbackByDefault: Bool; enum Key { static let captureShortcut, showMenuBarIcon, launchAtLogin, hideFromSharing, didFirstRun, didRequestScreenCapture, ownPlacementsCount, supportAskShown, clickThroughFallback: String }; static func register(in defaults: UserDefaults); static func storedValue(for shortcut: Shortcut?) -> [String: Int]; static func shortcut(fromStored value: Any?) -> Shortcut? }` and `extension UserDefaults { var captureShortcut: Shortcut?; var showMenuBarIcon: Bool; var launchAtLogin: Bool; var hideFromSharing: Bool; var didFirstRun: Bool; var didRequestScreenCapture: Bool; var ownPlacementsCount: Int; var supportAskShown: Bool; var clickThroughFallback: Bool }`.
- `App/SystemShortcuts.swift`: `struct Entry: Equatable { let keyCode: UInt32; let modifiers: UInt32; let enabled: Bool }`, `static let modifierMask: UInt32`, `static func entries(from table: [[String: Any]]) -> [Entry]`, `static func conflicts(with shortcut: Shortcut, in entries: [Entry]) -> Bool`, `static func liveTable() -> [[String: Any]]`, next to the contract's `conflicts(with:)`.
- `App/SingleInstance.swift`: `@MainActor enum SingleInstance { nonisolated static let showHomeNotification: Notification.Name; struct Copy: Equatable { let pid: Int32; let launchDate: Date? }; nonisolated static func incumbent(among others: [Copy], mine: Copy, now: Date = Date()) -> Copy?; static func runningIncumbent() -> NSRunningApplication?; static func yield(to incumbent: NSRunningApplication, userLaunch: Bool) -> Never; nonisolated static func isLoginLaunch(_ event: NSAppleEventDescriptor?) -> Bool }`.
- `App/LaunchPlan.swift`: `struct LaunchPlan: Equatable { var opensHome: Bool; var placesTutorials: Bool; var checksForUpdateAfterLogin: Bool; static let loginUpdateCheckDelay: TimeInterval; static func decide(loginLaunch: Bool, didFirstRun: Bool, libraryIsEmpty: Bool, readOnly: Bool) -> LaunchPlan }`.
- `App/LoginItem.swift`: `@MainActor protocol LoginItemService: AnyObject { var status: SMAppService.Status { get }; func register() throws; func unregister() throws; func openSystemSettings() }`, `LiveLoginItemService`, `@MainActor final class LoginItem { enum State: Equatable { case on, off, requiresApproval }; init(service: LoginItemService? = nil, defaults: UserDefaults = .standard); var state: State { get }; func applyAtLaunch(); func setEnabled(_ on: Bool); func openSystemSettings() }`.
- `Updater/Updater.swift`: `enum UpdateState: Equatable { case upToDate, available, downloading, updating, failed }`, `@MainActor final class Updater: ObservableObject` (constants, `struct Release`, `state`, `latestVersion`, `appVersion`, `init(appVersion:fetch:beforeHelper:)`, `isHomebrewManaged`, `isVersion(_:newerThan:)`, `release(from:)`, `liveFetch()`, `helperScript(brew:bundle:pid:)`, `brewPath()`, `check(completion:)`, `handle(_:)`, `performUpdate()`).
- `Home/`: `ShortcutRecording`, `ShortcutRecorderButton`, `ShortcutRecorder`, `HistoryList`, `protocol StickerSession` (+ `SessionCoordinator` conformance), `HistoryDeletion`, `ThumbnailCache`, `HistoryGrid`, `HomeModel`, `SettingsText`, `SettingsSection`, `AboutLinks`, `VersionCheck`, `AboutPopover`, `UpdateStrip`, `HomeView`, `HomeWindow`, `HomeWindowController`.
- `Onboarding/`: `TutorialLayout`, `TutorialArt`, `TutorialStickers`, `SupportAskRule`, `SupportAskPanel`, `SupportAsk`.
- `StatusItemController`: `var onOpenHome: (() -> Void)?`, `var isVisible: Bool`, `var buttonScreenFrame: CGRect? { get }`.
- `StickerManager.masterChanged(for sticker: UUID)`; `SessionCoordinator.showPermissionPanel()`; `AppDelegate.showHome(checkingForUpdate: Bool = true)` (internal: the main menu's ⌘, calls it).

Deviations from the spec's wording, all within its intent:

- **Single instance, in two halves.** The running copy is looked up in `applicationWillFinishLaunching`, before anything registers or appears. Whether the launch is a login launch (which decides whether the running copy is asked to open Home) can only be read inside `applicationDidFinishLaunching` (`currentAppleEvent` is nil earlier, as Pullcord's code notes), so the hand-over (post `com.ethan.stickermine.showHome`, activate the running copy, `exit(0)`) is the first thing `applicationDidFinishLaunching` does after the test-host guard and the debug `-styleGallery` check (M3's gallery never touches the library, so it may run next to the installed copy; it renders and quits). Nothing is registered or shown in between.
- **The main menu** (never visible in an agent app, but its key equivalents work while Stickermine is active): ⌘, opens Home (which holds the settings) instead of SwiftUI's empty Settings window, and there is no ⌘Q, as in Key54: quitting takes every sticker off the desktop, so it is always a deliberate click (Home's Quit or the menu bar menu's Quit). Home closes on ⌘W itself (`HomeWindow`), since the menu has no Close item.
- **The update strip** also shows "Downloading the update…" and "Updating to vX…" between Update and the relaunch, as Pullcord's does; it is hidden whenever the state is `upToDate`.
- **The tutorial cards are two lines** ("Press ⌃⌥⌘S / to make a sticker", "Double-click me to move, / resize or remove.", "Click the menu bar icon / to make a sticker"), and the three tutorials are turned slightly (−2°, +1.5°, +3°: the brand's "slight rotation between −4° and +3°"). Their exact positions, which the spec leaves to the plan, are `TutorialLayout`'s: right-aligned 24 pt in from the main display's visible frame, in the column right of Home's initial frame (at least 200 pt wide; on a small display Home starts smaller, down to its 560 × 480 minimum, so that the column fits and Home fits the visible height), the cards stacked from the top with a 16 pt gap, Maple at the bottom, each scaled down (never up) to a third of the display's height and the column's width.
- **Home's Screen Recording row** offers Relaunch Stickermine next to Turn On… once access was asked for and is still off (spec section 14's fallback for a stale in-process check, offered where the user comes back to).
- **The support ask** appears 0.5 s after the placement click (so that click's mouse-up is over) and is placed below the menu bar icon with its right edge on the icon's; while the icon is hidden, 8 pt in from the main display's top-right visible corner.
- **The recorder** refuses a chord without ⌘ or ⌃ (F-keys excepted) by beeping and showing why, and keeps listening; Pullcord's ends recording there. Shift doesn't make Esc or Delete a chord (⇧Esc still cancels), as in Pullcord.


## Global Constraints

All constraints in the index apply. Milestone-specific ones, verbatim from the spec:

- "**Hideable** in settings (`showMenuBarIcon`). Without it the app works through the shortcut; opening the app again brings up Home." and "**Right-click or Control-click:** a menu with New Sticker (showing the shortcut; disabled during a session), Open Stickermine…, Quit." (section 13)
- "**Single instance**, in `applicationWillFinishLaunching` before any window can appear: if another process with this bundle ID is running, the earlier launch wins. A user launch posts the distributed notification `com.ethan.stickermine.showHome` so the running copy opens Home, then exits. Ported from Pullcord's `yieldToRunningInstance`." (section 13)
- "**Launch at login:** `SMAppService.mainApp`, on by default (stickers only exist while the app runs). Register when on and `status != .enabled`; unregister when turned off. If `status == .requiresApproval`, the setting shows that and opens `SMAppService.openSystemSettingsLoginItems()`. Detect a login launch from the `kAEOpenApplication` Apple event's `keyAELaunchedAsLogInItem`, read inside `applicationDidFinishLaunching`." (section 13)
- "**What each launch does:** every launch restores stickers. **A launch that isn't a login launch** (double-clicking the app, Spotlight, Finder) also opens Home; on first run it also places the tutorial stickers. **A login launch** opens no windows, except that if the update check about 6 s later finds an update, Home opens to show the update strip (Pullcord's behaviour)." and "**Reopen** (`applicationShouldHandleReopen`) and the distributed notification open Home." (section 13)
- "**History grid:** every non-tutorial sticker, newest first. Click → picker panel (style row only) → placement. Right-click → Delete from History (with confirmation). A **New Sticker** button at the top." (section 13)
- "**Capture shortcut** recorder. Every chord must include ⌘ or ⌃ (F-keys excepted). Bare Esc cancels recording, bare Delete clears. Hotkeys are unregistered while recording; recording is cancelled when Home closes or resigns key. Warn when the chord matches an enabled system shortcut (`CopySymbolicHotKeys()`); clashes with other apps can't be detected (Carbon hotkeys are non-exclusive and register successfully anyway). Help mentions that VoiceOver users may want to rebind (VO-⌘-S)." (section 13)
- "**Show menu bar icon.** **Launch at login.** **Hide stickers from screen sharing and screenshots** (described as best-effort; also hides them from your own screenshots). **Screen Recording** status with a button to fix it. Re-checked when Home becomes key." (section 13)
- "**Footer:** an ⓘ About popover (icon, name, version line that doubles as Check for Updates, Website, Star on GitHub, Buy me a coffee → `https://ko-fi.com/grokcodile`, Report a Bug → GitHub issues) and **Quit**." and "**Update strip:** appears only when an update is available or failed." (section 13)
- "It polls `api.github.com/repos/grokcodile/stickermine/releases/latest` only on Home open and once about 6 s after a login launch, and offers a release only if its assets include `Stickermine.dmg`. If installed by the Homebrew cask (Caskroom entry and running from `/Applications/Stickermine.app`), it upgrades through a detached helper that kills by PID, runs `brew upgrade --cask`, clears quarantine and relaunches; otherwise it downloads the DMG to `~/Downloads` (HTTP 200 only) and opens it. 180 s watchdog. Before spawning the helper, it runs the termination cleanup in section 5.10. Keep `Info.plist`'s version ahead of the newest tag." (section 13)
- "Runs on a user launch when `didFirstRun` is false and `library.json` has no stickers. `TutorialStickers` renders three masters once, saves them as ordinary `stickers/<uuid>.png` entries under three fixed UUIDs defined in code, with `tutorial: true`, places them, and sets `didFirstRun` once the index is saved. They are never re-created after removal." (section 13)
- "The text cards are SF Pro Rounded on a sticker-shaped card in the brand colours, with their own white border. All three use the **Shadow** style … Maple's master is about 315 pt wide like on the website. Automatic placement scales each tutorial sticker to fit within a third of the main display's height and places them on the main display clear of Home's initial (centred) frame: the text cards stacked top-right, Maple bottom-right." and "Tutorial stickers don't appear in the history grid, don't count toward the support ask, and are deleted outright (PNG and entry) when removed." (section 13)
- "`ownPlacementsCount` goes up by one on each placement click of a non-tutorial sticker, fresh or re-placed from history; cross-display drags don't count and nothing decrements it. The first time it is ≥ 5 and `supportAskShown` is false, show a small sticker-styled card near the menu bar icon (top-right of the main display if the icon is hidden), without activating the app or taking focus from the frontmost app … `supportAskShown` is set when the card appears; any dismissal (either button, Esc, clicking elsewhere, quitting) counts as declined. It never appears again." (section 13)
- "**Settings** in `UserDefaults`: `captureShortcut` (`{k, m}`), `showMenuBarIcon` (true), `launchAtLogin` (true), `hideFromSharing` (true), `didFirstRun`, `didRequestScreenCapture`, `ownPlacementsCount`, `supportAskShown`, and the hidden `clickThroughFallback` (false; not shown in Home; set with `defaults write com.ethan.stickermine clickThroughFallback -bool true`; read at launch)." (section 12)
- "**Delete from history:** cancels any open session for that sticker, ends editing of any of its placements (discarding a gesture), removes the sticker, its PNG and every placement on every display (including disconnected ones). The confirmation states the number of placements. Every render completion checks that its placement still exists before committing." and "**Thumbnails** for the history grid: `CGImageSourceCreateThumbnailAtIndex` (max 256 px) into an in-memory `NSCache`." (section 12)
- "If it is newer than this build supports, show the stored placements and write nothing (no PNGs, no index); capture, editing and history changes are disabled, and Home shows "This library was saved by a newer Stickermine. Update to make changes."" (section 12)
- "**Termination and leftovers:** on `applicationWillTerminate`, the menu's Quit, and before the updater spawns its helper, terminate a running `screencapture` process, delete its temp file and flush the pending library save." (section 5.10)
- "If preflight is still false when the user comes back (the panel or Home becomes key), it also offers **Relaunch Stickermine** (a detached `open` of the app after quitting), in case the in-process preflight result is stale." (section 14)
- "**App icon:** a classic `AppIcon.appiconset` in `Stickermine/Assets.xcassets` with the 10 mac slices (16–512 @1x/@2x), the rounded tile fitted to the 824/1024 grid like Pullcord and Key54. The slices are generated by a committed script, `design/make-icon.sh`, and the generated PNGs are committed (release.yml has no icon step). The script renders `design/icon-pickaxe.svg` with `qlmanage -t -s 1024` (WebKit keeps the SVG's drop shadow; NSImage/CoreSVG drops it), then `sips -z` for each slice. … Check the result in Finder, the Dock and Login Items on macOS 26.x." (section 16)
- From M2–M5, unchanged: every display a test builds sits at x ≥ 40 000 pt, and every test that orders a window in closes it before it suspends or returns, so the test host never shows a window (`AppDelegateTests.testHostShowsNoWindows`); tests never activate Stickermine, never make a window key and never touch `UserDefaults.standard`, the real login items, TCC or the network (fakes stand in: `LoginItemTests.FakeService`, `HomeModelTests.FakeAccess`, an `Updater` with a canned `fetch`). `Settings.swift`, `SystemShortcuts.swift`, `LaunchPlan.swift` and `TutorialLayout.swift` never import AppKit or SwiftUI (`LibraryModelTests.pureLogicFilesStayFreeOfAppKit`, extended in Task 1).
- As in M1–M5: run every command from the repository root (`/Users/ethan/Developer/stickermine`) in bash or zsh, not fish. The test command is `DEVELOPER_DIR=/Applications/Xcode-beta.app/Contents/Developer xcodebuild test -project Stickermine.xcodeproj -scheme Stickermine -destination 'platform=macOS,arch=arm64' -derivedDataPath build/DerivedData`, with `-only-testing:StickermineTests/<Suite>` for one suite. Ignore the harmless lines the test host prints (`com.apple.linkd.autoShortcut`, `MDB_MAP_FULL`, `[VisionLog] PostProcessingOperation error`, M3's deliberate `Compiler failed to build request` / `program_source:` lines, `render of … failed: missingMaster`, and `appintentsmetadataprocessor … Metadata extraction skipped`).

## Review Focus

Owned from the index: **item 5, losing the way back to Home** ("menu bar icon hidden + shortcut cleared or conflicting. Expect: opening the app from Finder/Spotlight always opens Home; a cleared shortcut makes tutorial 1 say "Click the menu bar icon…" and the shortcut recorder refuses chords without ⌘ or ⌃"). Pinned by `LaunchPlanTests.aUserLaunchOpensHomeAndTheFirstOnePlacesTheTutorials` (every user launch opens Home), `AppDelegateTests.reopeningOpensHome` and `aSecondCopyHandsOverBeforeAnythingStarts` (reopen and a second launch reach Home), `TutorialStickersTests.tutorialOneSaysTheShortcutOrTheMenuBarIcon` and `aShortcutChangeRedrawsTutorialOneInPlace`, `ShortcutRecorderTests.outcomesFollowTheSpecRules` and `aChordWithoutCommandOrControlIsRefusedAndListeningGoesOn`, `HomeTextTests.theShortcutHelpCoversConflictsAndTheWayBack`, `SystemShortcutsTests`, and Task 16 rows 5–11. Shared from the index: **item 4, many stickers**: Task 16 row 25 repeats M5's 50-sticker launch check with the tutorial stickers on screen.

Milestone-specific failure modes:

1. **The capture hotkey stays suspended, or the old chord keeps firing, after recording.** Expect: the hotkey is unregistered from the first moment the recorder listens until it stops, and whatever stops it (a chord, Esc, Delete, a click into another app, closing Home, a second recorder) brings back exactly the stored shortcut. Pinned by `ShortcutRecorderTests.recordingSuspendsTheHotkeyAndAChordReplacesTheShortcut`, `escCancelsAndDeleteClears`, `onlyOneRecorderListensAtATime`, `leavingTheWindowStopsListening`, `HomeWindowControllerTests.resigningKeyOrClosingStopsTheRecorder`, `HomeModelTests.aRecordedShortcutIsStoredCheckedAndPassedOn`, and Task 16 rows 4–8.
2. **Quitting or updating loses the last edit or leaves a capture behind.** Expect: Quit (menu or Home), `applicationWillTerminate` and the updater (before its helper's `kill`, which skips `applicationWillTerminate`) all cancel the session (terminating `screencapture` and deleting its temporary file), decline a support ask that is up, and flush the library. Pinned by `AppDelegateTests.quitTerminationAndTheUpdaterRunTheSameCleanup`, `UpdaterTests.theHelperQuitsByPidUpgradesClearsQuarantineAndReopens`, and Task 16 row 24.
3. **The first run goes wrong:** tutorials made twice, placed under Home, shown in the history, counted towards the support ask, made again after being removed, or made for a newer library. Pinned by `TutorialStickersTests.theFirstRunSavesPlacesAndRemembersTheTutorials`, `removingATutorialDeletesItOutright`, `aNewerLibraryGetsNoTutorials`, `TutorialLayoutTests.everyStickerFitsAThirdOfTheHeightAndTheColumnBesideHome`, `aSmallDisplayGetsASmallerHomeThatTheTutorialsClear`, `thePosesDrawExactlyWhereTheLayoutSays`, `SupportAskTests.tutorialPlacementsDontCount`, `LaunchPlanTests.aLibraryWithStickersOrFromANewerBuildGetsNoTutorials`, and Task 16 rows 1–3.

### Task 1: Settings: the keys, their defaults and typed accessors

**Files:**
- Create: `Stickermine/App/Settings.swift`
- Modify: `Stickermine/App/StickermineApp.swift` (`SwiftUI.Settings`), `Stickermine/App/AppDelegate.swift` (its `clickThroughFallbackByDefault` reads the spike's value from `Settings`; register the defaults; read the two launch settings through `Settings`)
- Test: `StickermineTests/SettingsTests.swift` (create), `StickermineTests/LibraryModelTests.swift` (modify: the AppKit-free list)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Shortcut` and `Shortcut.defaultCapture` (M4), the value of `AppDelegate.clickThroughFallbackByDefault` (M2's spike decision, copied into `Settings` in Step 3), `PermissionPanel.didRequestKey` (M4, the same string as `Settings.Key.didRequestScreenCapture`).
- Produces (pure, Foundation only):

```text
enum Settings {
    static let clickThroughFallbackByDefault: Bool                     // the spike's decision (M2 Task 12 Step 5); false unless it chose the fallback
    enum Key { static let captureShortcut, showMenuBarIcon, launchAtLogin, hideFromSharing, didFirstRun,
               didRequestScreenCapture, ownPlacementsCount, supportAskShown, clickThroughFallback: String }
    static func register(in defaults: UserDefaults)                       // the contract's
    static func storedValue(for shortcut: Shortcut?) -> [String: Int]     // ["k": code, "m": mask]; [:] for none
    static func shortcut(fromStored value: Any?) -> Shortcut?             // nil for [:] and anything unreadable
}
extension UserDefaults {
    var captureShortcut: Shortcut?            // nil once cleared; ⌃⌥⌘S before anything was stored
    var showMenuBarIcon: Bool; var launchAtLogin: Bool; var hideFromSharing: Bool   // default true
    var didFirstRun: Bool; var didRequestScreenCapture: Bool; var supportAskShown: Bool   // default false
    var ownPlacementsCount: Int               // default 0
    var clickThroughFallback: Bool            // hidden; default Settings.clickThroughFallbackByDefault
}
extension AppDelegate { nonisolated static let clickThroughFallbackByDefault: Bool }   // contract change 1: = Settings.clickThroughFallbackByDefault
```

```swift
/// Every test uses its own suite, never `.standard`.
@MainActor
struct SettingsTests {

    @Test func registersTheSpecDefaultsWithoutWritingThem() throws {
        let (defaults, name) = suite()

        #expect(defaults.showMenuBarIcon)

        #expect(defaults.hideFromSharing)
```

Expected: `clickThroughFallbackByDefault = false`, unless the spike's Decisions table made the fallback the default. If it prints `clickThroughFallbackByDefault = true`, write `static let clickThroughFallbackByDefault = true` in the file below instead of `= false`; every other line stays as shown.

`Stickermine/App/Settings.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import Foundation

/// The settings Stickermine keeps in `UserDefaults` (spec section 12): their
/// key names, their defaults and typed accessors on `UserDefaults`. The app
/// calls `register(in: .standard)` first thing at launch; tests pass
/// `UserDefaults(suiteName:)`, never `.standard`.
enum Settings {
    /// From design/spike-results.md, Decisions: "Alpha click-through works, or
    /// the fallback mode becomes the default?". `defaults write
    /// com.ethan.stickermine clickThroughFallback -bool …` overrides it.
    /// (Here rather than in the app delegate, so this pure file never depends
    /// on the AppKit shell; the delegate's constant of the same name reads it.)
    static let clickThroughFallbackByDefault = false

    /// The key names, exactly as `defaults read com.ethan.stickermine` shows them.
    enum Key {
        /// `{k, m}`: the Carbon key code and modifier mask; `{}` once cleared.
        static let captureShortcut = "captureShortcut"
        static let showMenuBarIcon = "showMenuBarIcon"
        static let launchAtLogin = "launchAtLogin"
        static let hideFromSharing = "hideFromSharing"
        static let didFirstRun = "didFirstRun"
        /// The key `PermissionPanel.didRequestKey` names.
        static let didRequestScreenCapture = "didRequestScreenCapture"
        static let ownPlacementsCount = "ownPlacementsCount"
        static let supportAskShown = "supportAskShown"
        /// Hidden: never shown in Home. `defaults write com.ethan.stickermine
        /// clickThroughFallback -bool true`, read at launch (spec section 9).
        static let clickThroughFallback = "clickThroughFallback"
    }

    /// Every key's default. Registered defaults are never written to disk, so
    /// `defaults read com.ethan.stickermine` shows only what was changed.
    static func register(in defaults: UserDefaults) {
        defaults.register(defaults: [
            Key.captureShortcut: storedValue(for: .defaultCapture),
            Key.showMenuBarIcon: true,
            Key.launchAtLogin: true,
            Key.hideFromSharing: true,
            Key.didFirstRun: false,
            Key.didRequestScreenCapture: false,
            Key.ownPlacementsCount: 0,
            Key.supportAskShown: false,
            Key.clickThroughFallback: clickThroughFallbackByDefault,
        ])
    }

    /// How a shortcut is stored: `{"k": keyCode, "m": modifiers}`, or an empty
    /// dictionary for "no shortcut", so a cleared shortcut never falls back to
    /// the registered ⌃⌥⌘S.
    static func storedValue(for shortcut: Shortcut?) -> [String: Int] {
        guard let shortcut else { return [:] }
        return ["k": Int(shortcut.keyCode), "m": Int(shortcut.modifiers)]
    }

    /// The shortcut a stored value stands for: nil for a cleared one and for
    /// anything this build can't read.
    static func shortcut(fromStored value: Any?) -> Shortcut? {
        guard let dictionary = value as? [String: Any],
              let keyCode = dictionary["k"] as? Int, let modifiers = dictionary["m"] as? Int,
              (0...0xFFFF).contains(keyCode), (0...0xFFFF).contains(modifiers)
        else { return nil }
        return Shortcut(keyCode: UInt32(keyCode), modifiers: UInt32(modifiers))
    }
}

/// Typed accessors. Each getter falls back to the spec's default when the key
/// is missing, so a read before `Settings.register(in:)` still gets it.
extension UserDefaults {
    /// The capture shortcut; nil once the user cleared it.
    var captureShortcut: Shortcut? {
        get {
            guard let value = object(forKey: Settings.Key.captureShortcut) else { return .defaultCapture }
            return Settings.shortcut(fromStored: value)
        }
        set { set(Settings.storedValue(for: newValue), forKey: Settings.Key.captureShortcut) }
    }

    var showMenuBarIcon: Bool {
        get { object(forKey: Settings.Key.showMenuBarIcon) as? Bool ?? true }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.showMenuBarIcon) }
    }

    var launchAtLogin: Bool {
        get { object(forKey: Settings.Key.launchAtLogin) as? Bool ?? true }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.launchAtLogin) }
    }

    var hideFromSharing: Bool {
        get { object(forKey: Settings.Key.hideFromSharing) as? Bool ?? true }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.hideFromSharing) }
    }

    var didFirstRun: Bool {
        get { bool(forKey: Settings.Key.didFirstRun) }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.didFirstRun) }
    }

    var didRequestScreenCapture: Bool {
        get { bool(forKey: Settings.Key.didRequestScreenCapture) }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.didRequestScreenCapture) }
    }

    var ownPlacementsCount: Int {
        get { integer(forKey: Settings.Key.ownPlacementsCount) }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.ownPlacementsCount) }
    }

    var supportAskShown: Bool {
        get { bool(forKey: Settings.Key.supportAskShown) }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.supportAskShown) }
    }

    /// Read once at launch; a change takes effect on the next launch.
    var clickThroughFallback: Bool {
        get { object(forKey: Settings.Key.clickThroughFallback) as? Bool ?? Settings.clickThroughFallbackByDefault }
        set { set(newValue, forKey: Settings.Key.clickThroughFallback) }
    }
}
```

Inside the module, `Settings` now names the enum above, so the scene must be spelled `SwiftUI.Settings` (contract change 2).

`Stickermine/App/StickermineApp.swift` (complete new version; it replaces the whole file):

```swift
import SwiftUI

/// The SwiftUI entry point. Its only scene is an empty Settings scene, so SwiftUI
/// never opens a window of its own: every window in Stickermine is an AppKit
/// window or panel owned by a controller that AppDelegate holds (spec section 4).
/// (`SwiftUI.Settings`, because the app's own `Settings` enum holds its
/// UserDefaults keys.)
@main
struct StickermineApp: App {
    @NSApplicationDelegateAdaptor(AppDelegate.self) private var appDelegate

    var body: some Scene {
        SwiftUI.Settings {
            EmptyView()
        }
    }
}
```

### Task 2: SystemShortcuts: is this chord one of macOS's own shortcuts?

**Files:**
- Create: `Stickermine/App/SystemShortcuts.swift`
- Test: `StickermineTests/SystemShortcutsTests.swift` (create)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Shortcut` (M4).
- Produces (pure apart from `liveTable()`, no AppKit):

```text
enum SystemShortcuts {
    struct Entry: Equatable { let keyCode: UInt32; let modifiers: UInt32; let enabled: Bool }
    static let modifierMask: UInt32                                       // ⌘ ⇧ ⌥ ⌃ (0x1B00)
    static func entries(from table: [[String: Any]]) -> [Entry]          // kHISymbolicHotKeyCode/Modifiers/Enabled rows
    static func conflicts(with shortcut: Shortcut, in entries: [Entry]) -> Bool
    static func liveTable() -> [[String: Any]]                            // CopySymbolicHotKeys
    static func conflicts(with s: Shortcut) -> Bool                       // the contract's: the live table
}
```

What the table looks like (read on this Mac, macOS 27.2): 233 rows, each a dictionary with `kHISymbolicHotKeyCode`, `kHISymbolicHotKeyModifiers` and `kHISymbolicHotKeyEnabled` (NSNumber values). F-key rows carry the fn bit 0x20000 (F11 Show Desktop is code 103, modifiers 0x20000), which a `Shortcut` never has, so only the four modifier bits are compared. Disabled rows (for example the accessibility zoom chords with ⌃⌥⌘, 0x1900) never conflict.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`StickermineTests/SystemShortcutsTests.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import Carbon.HIToolbox
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import Stickermine

/// An injected table stands in for `CopySymbolicHotKeys`, with rows shaped
/// like the real ones (NSNumber values under the kHISymbolicHotKey* keys).
struct SystemShortcutsTests {
    private func row(_ code: Int, _ modifiers: Int, enabled: Bool? = true) -> [String: Any] {
        var row: [String: Any] = [kHISymbolicHotKeyCode as String: NSNumber(value: code),
                                  kHISymbolicHotKeyModifiers as String: NSNumber(value: modifiers)]
        if let enabled { row[kHISymbolicHotKeyEnabled as String] = NSNumber(value: enabled) }
        return row
    }

    @Test func anEnabledRowWithTheSameKeyAndModifiersConflicts() {
        // ⌘⇧3 (key 20, 0x300): Save picture of screen as a file.
        let entries = SystemShortcuts.entries(from: [row(20, 0x300), row(49, 0x100)])
        #expect(SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: Shortcut(keyCode: 20, modifiers: UInt32(cmdKey | shiftKey)), in: entries))
        #expect(SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: Shortcut(keyCode: 49, modifiers: UInt32(cmdKey)), in: entries))
    }

    @Test func aDisabledOrMissingEnabledFlagDoesNotConflict() {
        let entries = SystemShortcuts.entries(from: [row(1, 0x1900, enabled: false), row(2, 0x1100, enabled: nil)])
        #expect(!SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: .defaultCapture, in: entries))
        #expect(!SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: Shortcut(keyCode: 2, modifiers: UInt32(cmdKey | controlKey)), in: entries))
    }

    @Test func otherModifiersOrKeysDoNotConflict() {
        let entries = SystemShortcuts.entries(from: [row(20, 0x300)])
        #expect(!SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: Shortcut(keyCode: 20, modifiers: UInt32(cmdKey)), in: entries))
        #expect(!SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: Shortcut(keyCode: 21, modifiers: UInt32(cmdKey | shiftKey)), in: entries))
        #expect(!SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: .defaultCapture, in: entries))
    }

    @Test func theFnBitOnFKeyRowsIsIgnored() {
        // F11 (103) with the fn bit 0x20000: Show Desktop.
        let entries = SystemShortcuts.entries(from: [row(103, 0x20000)])
        #expect(entries == [SystemShortcuts.Entry(keyCode: 103, modifiers: 0, enabled: true)])
        #expect(SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: Shortcut(keyCode: 103, modifiers: 0), in: entries))
        #expect(!SystemShortcuts.conflicts(with: Shortcut(keyCode: 103, modifiers: UInt32(shiftKey)), in: entries))
    }

    @Test func malformedRowsAreSkipped() {
        let table: [[String: Any]] = [
            [kHISymbolicHotKeyCode as String: NSNumber(value: 1)],
            [kHISymbolicHotKeyModifiers as String: NSNumber(value: 0x100)],
            [kHISymbolicHotKeyCode as String: "S", kHISymbolicHotKeyModifiers as String: NSNumber(value: 0x100)],
            row(-1, 0x100),
            row(1, 0x1900),
        ]
        #expect(SystemShortcuts.entries(from: table) == [SystemShortcuts.Entry(keyCode: 1, modifiers: 0x1900, enabled: true)])
    }

    @Test func theLiveTableCanBeRead() {
        // Every Mac has system shortcuts (Spotlight, screenshots, Mission Control).
        #expect(!SystemShortcuts.entries(from: SystemShortcuts.liveTable()).isEmpty)
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `SystemShortcuts`**

`Stickermine/App/SystemShortcuts.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import Carbon.HIToolbox
import Foundation

/// Whether a chord is one of macOS's own enabled keyboard shortcuts (System
/// Settings › Keyboard › Keyboard Shortcuts), read with `CopySymbolicHotKeys`
/// (spec section 13). The recorder warns about such a chord but still takes
/// it. Clashes with other apps can't be found: Carbon hotkeys aren't
/// exclusive, and registering one that another app uses succeeds anyway.
enum SystemShortcuts {
    /// One row of the symbolic hotkey table.
    struct Entry: Equatable {
        let keyCode: UInt32
        /// Only the four modifiers a `Shortcut` carries.
        let modifiers: UInt32
        let enabled: Bool
    }

    /// ⌘ ⇧ ⌥ ⌃. The table sets other bits too: F-key rows carry the fn bit
    /// (0x20000, `kEventKeyModifierFnMask`), which a `Shortcut` never has.
    static let modifierMask = UInt32(cmdKey | shiftKey | optionKey | controlKey)

    /// The table's rows; rows without a key code or modifiers are skipped.
    static func entries(from table: [[String: Any]]) -> [Entry] {
        table.compactMap { row in
            guard let code = (row[kHISymbolicHotKeyCode as String] as? NSNumber)?.intValue,
                  let modifiers = (row[kHISymbolicHotKeyModifiers as String] as? NSNumber)?.intValue,
                  (0...0xFFFF).contains(code), modifiers >= 0
            else { return nil }
            let enabled = (row[kHISymbolicHotKeyEnabled as String] as? NSNumber)?.boolValue ?? false
            return Entry(keyCode: UInt32(code), modifiers: UInt32(truncatingIfNeeded: modifiers) & modifierMask,
                         enabled: enabled)
        }
    }

    /// An enabled row with the same key and the same four modifiers.
    static func conflicts(with shortcut: Shortcut, in entries: [Entry]) -> Bool {
        let modifiers = shortcut.modifiers & modifierMask
        return entries.contains { $0.enabled && $0.keyCode == shortcut.keyCode && $0.modifiers == modifiers }
    }

    /// The live table (empty if the call fails).
    static func liveTable() -> [[String: Any]] {
        var table: Unmanaged<CFArray>?
        guard CopySymbolicHotKeys(&table) == noErr,
              let rows = table?.takeRetainedValue() as? [[String: Any]]
        else { return [] }
        return rows
    }

    /// The contract's check, against the live table.
    static func conflicts(with shortcut: Shortcut) -> Bool {
        conflicts(with: shortcut, in: entries(from: liveTable()))
    }
}
```

### Task 3: ShortcutRecorder: the capture-shortcut field

**Files:**
- Create: `Stickermine/Home/ShortcutRecorder.swift` (the first file in the new `Home/` folder; the synchronized `Stickermine/` group picks it up)
- Test: `StickermineTests/ShortcutRecorderTests.swift` (create)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Shortcut`, `Shortcut.isAcceptable` and `Shortcut.label` (M4).
- Produces:

```text
enum ShortcutRecording {                                                  // pure
    enum Outcome: Equatable { case cancel, clear, reject, record(Shortcut) }
    static func carbonModifiers(_ flags: NSEvent.ModifierFlags) -> UInt32
    static func outcome(keyCode: UInt16, flags: NSEvent.ModifierFlags) -> Outcome
}
@MainActor final class ShortcutRecorderButton: NSButton {
    private(set) static weak var active: ShortcutRecorderButton?          // the one listening
    static let listeningTitle: String                                      // "Type a shortcut…"
    static let emptyTitle: String                                          // "Record Shortcut"
    var shortcut: Shortcut?
    var onChange: ((Shortcut?) -> Void)?                                   // runs before the hotkeys resume
    var onRecordingChange: ((Bool) -> Void)?                               // true: suspend the hotkey; false: resume
    var onRejected: (() -> Void)?
    private(set) var isRecording: Bool
    init(shortcut: Shortcut?)
    func beginRecording(); @discardableResult func handle(_ event: NSEvent) -> Bool; func cancelRecording()
}
struct ShortcutRecorder: NSViewRepresentable {                             // SwiftUI wrapper for Home
    let shortcut: Shortcut?; let onChange: (Shortcut?) -> Void
    let onRecordingChange: (Bool) -> Void; let onRejected: () -> Void
}
```

Pullcord's `RecorderButton` rules, kept: one recorder listens at a time; a local `.keyDown` monitor swallows every key while listening; "bare" means no ⌘, ⌥ or ⌃ (Shift allowed), so bare Esc cancels and bare Delete or Forward Delete clears; the hotkeys are parked while listening and come back however listening stops; Home cancels it when it closes or resigns key (Task 13). Changed for the spec: the modifier rule is `Shortcut.isAcceptable` (⌘ or ⌃, or an F-key), and a refused chord beeps and keeps listening.

```swift
@MainActor
struct ShortcutRecorderTests {
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the recorder**

`Stickermine/Home/ShortcutRecorder.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import AppKit
import Carbon.HIToolbox
import SwiftUI

/// What one key press does while the recorder listens (spec section 13),
/// ported from Pullcord's RecorderButton. Pure.
enum ShortcutRecording {
    enum Outcome: Equatable {
        /// Bare Esc: stop listening, keep the shortcut.
        case cancel
        /// Bare Delete or Forward Delete: no shortcut.
        case clear
        /// A chord without ⌘ or ⌃ that isn't an F-key: refused, keep listening.
        case reject
        case record(Shortcut)
    }

    /// The Carbon mask for the four modifiers a shortcut can carry.
    static func carbonModifiers(_ flags: NSEvent.ModifierFlags) -> UInt32 {
        var mask: UInt32 = 0
        if flags.contains(.command) { mask |= UInt32(cmdKey) }
        if flags.contains(.shift) { mask |= UInt32(shiftKey) }
        if flags.contains(.option) { mask |= UInt32(optionKey) }
        if flags.contains(.control) { mask |= UInt32(controlKey) }
        return mask
    }

    /// "Bare" allows Shift, as in Pullcord: ⇧Esc still cancels.
    static func outcome(keyCode: UInt16, flags: NSEvent.ModifierFlags) -> Outcome {
        let modifiers = carbonModifiers(flags)
        let bare = modifiers & ~UInt32(shiftKey) == 0
        let code = Int(keyCode)
        if bare && code == kVK_Escape { return .cancel }
        if bare && (code == kVK_Delete || code == kVK_ForwardDelete) { return .clear }
        let shortcut = Shortcut(keyCode: UInt32(keyCode), modifiers: modifiers)
        return shortcut.isAcceptable ? .record(shortcut) : .reject
    }
}

/// The capture-shortcut field: click to record. While it listens, the global
/// hotkey is suspended (`onRecordingChange(true)`) so the chord being typed
/// can't start a capture; bare Esc cancels, bare Delete clears, and a chord
/// without ⌘ or ⌃ (F-keys excepted) beeps and keeps listening. One recorder
/// listens at a time; Home cancels it when it closes or resigns key.
@MainActor
final class ShortcutRecorderButton: NSButton {
    /// The recorder that is listening, if any.
    private(set) static weak var active: ShortcutRecorderButton?

    static let listeningTitle = "Type a shortcut…"
    static let emptyTitle = "Record Shortcut"

    var shortcut: Shortcut? {
        didSet { updateTitle() }
    }
    /// A recorded chord, or nil after bare Delete. Runs before the hotkeys resume.
    var onChange: ((Shortcut?) -> Void)?
    /// True when listening starts, false when it stops (the hotkeys suspend and resume).
    var onRecordingChange: ((Bool) -> Void)?
    /// A refused chord (the button beeps).
    var onRejected: (() -> Void)?

    private(set) var isRecording = false
    private var monitor: Any?

    init(shortcut: Shortcut?) {
        self.shortcut = shortcut
        super.init(frame: NSRect(x: 0, y: 0, width: 160, height: 28))
        bezelStyle = .rounded
        setButtonType(.momentaryPushIn)
        target = self
        action = #selector(clicked(_:))
        updateTitle()
    }

    required init?(coder: NSCoder) {
        fatalError("ShortcutRecorderButton is only created in code")
    }

    @objc private func clicked(_ sender: Any?) {
        if isRecording { cancelRecording() } else { beginRecording() }
    }

    func beginRecording() {
        Self.active?.cancelRecording()
        guard !isRecording else { return }
        isRecording = true
        Self.active = self
        updateTitle()
        onRecordingChange?(true)
        monitor = NSEvent.addLocalMonitorForEvents(matching: .keyDown) { [weak self] event in
            guard let self, self.handle(event) else { return event }
            return nil
        }
    }

    /// One key press while listening; true when it was used (and swallowed).
    @discardableResult
    func handle(_ event: NSEvent) -> Bool {
        guard isRecording, event.type == .keyDown else { return false }
        switch ShortcutRecording.outcome(keyCode: event.keyCode, flags: event.modifierFlags) {
        case .cancel:
            cancelRecording()
        case .clear:
            finish(with: nil)
        case .reject:
            NSSound.beep()
            onRejected?()
        case .record(let recorded):
            finish(with: recorded)
        }
        return true
    }

    /// Stops listening and keeps the shortcut. Safe to call when not listening.
    func cancelRecording() {
        stopListening()
    }

    private func finish(with recorded: Shortcut?) {
        shortcut = recorded
        // The new shortcut is stored and registered (suspended) before the
        // hotkeys resume, so the old chord never comes back in between.
        onChange?(recorded)
        stopListening()
    }

    private func stopListening() {
        guard isRecording else { return }
        if let monitor { NSEvent.removeMonitor(monitor) }
        monitor = nil
        isRecording = false
        if Self.active === self { Self.active = nil }
        updateTitle()
        onRecordingChange?(false)
    }

    override func viewWillMove(toWindow newWindow: NSWindow?) {
        if newWindow == nil { cancelRecording() }
        super.viewWillMove(toWindow: newWindow)
    }

    private func updateTitle() {
        title = isRecording ? Self.listeningTitle : (shortcut?.label ?? Self.emptyTitle)
    }
}

/// The recorder in SwiftUI (Home's settings section).
struct ShortcutRecorder: NSViewRepresentable {
    let shortcut: Shortcut?
    let onChange: (Shortcut?) -> Void
    let onRecordingChange: (Bool) -> Void
    let onRejected: () -> Void

    func makeNSView(context: Context) -> ShortcutRecorderButton {
        let button = ShortcutRecorderButton(shortcut: shortcut)
        button.setContentHuggingPriority(.required, for: .horizontal)
        return button
    }

    func updateNSView(_ button: ShortcutRecorderButton, context: Context) {
        if !button.isRecording, button.shortcut != shortcut { button.shortcut = shortcut }
        button.onChange = onChange
        button.onRecordingChange = onRecordingChange
        button.onRejected = onRejected
    }
}
```

```text
@MainActor enum SingleInstance {
    nonisolated static let showHomeNotification: Notification.Name       // "com.ethan.stickermine.showHome"
    struct Copy: Equatable { let pid: Int32; let launchDate: Date? }
    nonisolated static func incumbent(among others: [Copy], mine: Copy, now: Date = Date()) -> Copy?   // pure

    nonisolated static func isLoginLaunch(_ event: NSAppleEventDescriptor?) -> Bool    // pure
}

    var opensHome: Bool; var placesTutorials: Bool; var checksForUpdateAfterLogin: Bool
    static let loginUpdateCheckDelay: TimeInterval                        // 6
    static func decide(loginLaunch: Bool, didFirstRun: Bool, libraryIsEmpty: Bool, readOnly: Bool) -> LaunchPlan
}
```

Ported from Pullcord's `yieldToRunningInstance` (main.swift line 1105) and `launchedAsLoginItem` (1148), with Stickermine's notification name. Pullcord runs the check first thing in `applicationDidFinishLaunching`; the spec puts it in `applicationWillFinishLaunching`, so Task 14 looks the running copy up there and hands over at the top of `applicationDidFinishLaunching`, where the login flag can be read (see the deviations above).

```swift
import AppKit
import Carbon
import Testing
@testable import Stickermine

struct SingleInstanceTests {
    private let now = Date(timeIntervalSince1970: 1_790_000_000)

    @Test func theEarlierLaunchStays() {
        let mine = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 500, launchDate: now)
        let older = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 900, launchDate: now.addingTimeInterval(-60))
        let newer = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 300, launchDate: now.addingTimeInterval(5))
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [older], mine: mine, now: now) == older)
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [newer], mine: mine, now: now) == nil)
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [newer, older], mine: mine, now: now) == older)
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [], mine: mine, now: now) == nil)
    }

    @Test func aTieGoesToTheLowerPidSoBothCopiesAgree() {
        let low = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 300, launchDate: now)
        let high = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 700, launchDate: now)
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [low], mine: high, now: now) == low)
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [high], mine: low, now: now) == nil)
    }

    @Test func unknownDatesLeanTowardsYielding() {
        // Started by running the executable directly: no launch date of its own.
        let undated = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 500, launchDate: nil)
        let other = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 900, launchDate: now.addingTimeInterval(-1))
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [other], mine: undated, now: now) == other)
        // Another copy without a date counts as long running.
        let mine = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 500, launchDate: now)
        let undatedOther = SingleInstance.Copy(pid: 900, launchDate: nil)
        #expect(SingleInstance.incumbent(among: [undatedOther], mine: mine, now: now) == undatedOther)
    }

    @Test func theNotificationNameIsTheSpecs() {
        #expect(SingleInstance.showHomeNotification.rawValue == "com.ethan.stickermine.showHome")
    }

    @Test func aLoginLaunchIsReadFromTheOpenApplicationEvent() {
        func event(_ id: AEEventID, loginItem: Bool) -> NSAppleEventDescriptor {
            let event = NSAppleEventDescriptor(eventClass: AEEventClass(kCoreEventClass), eventID: id,
                                               targetDescriptor: nil, returnID: AEReturnID(kAutoGenerateReturnID),
                                               transactionID: AETransactionID(kAnyTransactionID))
            if loginItem {
                event.setParam(NSAppleEventDescriptor(enumCode: OSType(keyAELaunchedAsLogInItem)),
                               forKeyword: AEKeyword(keyAEPropData))

        #expect(!SingleInstance.isLoginLaunch(event(AEEventID(kAEOpenApplication), loginItem: false)))
        #expect(!SingleInstance.isLoginLaunch(event(AEEventID(kAEReopenApplication), loginItem: true)))
        #expect(!SingleInstance.isLoginLaunch(nil))
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `SingleInstance`**

`Stickermine/App/SingleInstance.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import AppKit
import Carbon

/// One copy of Stickermine at a time (spec section 13), ported from
/// Pullcord's `yieldToRunningInstance`. Two copies would put every sticker on
/// the desktop twice and fight over ⌃⌥⌘S.
///
/// It asks which processes run with this bundle ID rather than setting
/// `LSMultipleInstancesProhibited`: LaunchServices keys that on the bundle on
/// disk, and a rebuilt bundle looks like a different app to it, while the old
/// copy still answers to the bundle ID.
@MainActor
enum SingleInstance {
    /// A duplicate launched by the user posts this on its way out; the copy
    /// that stays opens Home, so opening the app never looks like nothing happened.
    nonisolated static let showHomeNotification = Notification.Name("com.ethan.stickermine.showHome")

    /// A running copy: its process ID and launch date (nil when unknown).
    struct Copy: Equatable {
        let pid: Int32
        let launchDate: Date?
    }

    /// The copy this one yields to, or nil when this one stays. The earliest
    /// launch wins and an identical date goes to the lower pid, so two copies
    /// launched together reach the same answer. Both unknowns lean towards
    /// yielding: an unknown own date counts as "just now", an unknown other
    /// date as "long ago".
    nonisolated static func incumbent(among others: [Copy], mine: Copy, now: Date = Date()) -> Copy? {
        let launched = mine.launchDate ?? now
        return others.first { other in
            let theirs = other.launchDate ?? .distantPast
            return theirs == launched ? other.pid < mine.pid : theirs < launched
        }
    }

    /// The running copy this launch must hand over to, read in
    /// `applicationWillFinishLaunching`, before anything is registered.
    static func runningIncumbent() -> NSRunningApplication? {
        guard let bundleID = Bundle.main.bundleIdentifier else { return nil }
        let mine = ProcessInfo.processInfo.processIdentifier
        let others = NSRunningApplication.runningApplications(withBundleIdentifier: bundleID)
            .filter { $0.processIdentifier != mine && !$0.isTerminated }
        guard !others.isEmpty else { return nil }
        let copies = others.map { Copy(pid: $0.processIdentifier, launchDate: $0.launchDate) }
        guard let chosen = incumbent(among: copies, mine: Copy(pid: mine, launchDate: NSRunningApplication.current.launchDate))
        else { return nil }
        return others.first { $0.processIdentifier == chosen.pid }
    }

    /// Hands over and exits. A launch the user asked for means they wanted a
    /// window, so the running copy is told to open Home and brought forward; a
    /// login launch just goes. `exit` rather than `terminate`: nothing is
    /// registered yet, so there is nothing to unwind.
    static func yield(to incumbent: NSRunningApplication, userLaunch: Bool) -> Never {
        if userLaunch {
            DistributedNotificationCenter.default().postNotificationName(
                showHomeNotification, object: nil, userInfo: nil, deliverImmediately: true)
            incumbent.activate()
        }
        exit(0)
    }

    /// Whether launchd started this copy as a login item: the launch Apple
    /// event (`kAEOpenApplication`) carries `keyAELaunchedAsLogInItem` only
    /// then (spec section 13). Only readable inside
    /// `applicationDidFinishLaunching`; `currentAppleEvent` is nil earlier.
    nonisolated static func isLoginLaunch(_ event: NSAppleEventDescriptor?) -> Bool {
        guard let event, event.eventID == AEEventID(kAEOpenApplication) else { return false }
        return event.paramDescriptor(forKeyword: AEKeyword(keyAEPropData))?.enumCodeValue
            == OSType(keyAELaunchedAsLogInItem)
    }
}
```

`Stickermine/App/LaunchPlan.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import Foundation

/// What a launch does besides restoring the stickers, which every launch
/// does (spec section 13). Pure.
struct LaunchPlan: Equatable {
    /// A launch that isn't a login launch (Finder, Spotlight, `open`) opens Home.
    var opensHome: Bool
    /// First run: a user launch with `didFirstRun` false and no stickers in
    /// the library (never for a library saved by a newer build).
    var placesTutorials: Bool
    /// A login launch opens nothing, except that it checks for an update
    /// `loginUpdateCheckDelay` later and opens Home when one is found.
    var checksForUpdateAfterLogin: Bool

    /// After the login rush, so whichever login item activates next doesn't bury Home.
    static let loginUpdateCheckDelay: TimeInterval = 6

    static func decide(loginLaunch: Bool, didFirstRun: Bool, libraryIsEmpty: Bool, readOnly: Bool) -> LaunchPlan {
        if loginLaunch {
            return LaunchPlan(opensHome: false, placesTutorials: false, checksForUpdateAfterLogin: true)
        }
        return LaunchPlan(opensHome: true, placesTutorials: !didFirstRun && libraryIsEmpty && !readOnly,
                          checksForUpdateAfterLogin: false)
    }
}
```

```swift
import Foundation
import ServiceManagement
import Testing
@testable import Stickermine

/// A fake service: the real login items are never touched.
@MainActor
struct LoginItemTests {
    final class FakeService: LoginItemService {
        var status: SMAppService.Status
        var statusAfterRegister: SMAppService.Status = .enabled
        var registers = 0
        var unregisters = 0
        var settingsOpened = 0
        var failsToRegister = false

        init(status: SMAppService.Status) { self.status = status }

        func register() throws {
            registers += 1
            if failsToRegister { throw CocoaError(.featureUnsupported) }
            status = statusAfterRegister
        }

        func unregister() throws {
            unregisters += 1
            status = .notRegistered
        }

        func openSystemSettings() { settingsOpened += 1 }
    }

    private func suite() -> (UserDefaults, String) {
        let name = "StickermineTests-\(UUID().uuidString)"
        return (UserDefaults(suiteName: name)!, name)
    }

    @Test func launchRegistersWhenOnAndNotYetEnabled() {
        let (defaults, name) = suite()
        defer { defaults.removePersistentDomain(forName: name) }
        let service = FakeService(status: .notRegistered)
        let item = LoginItem(service: service, defaults: defaults)
        item.applyAtLaunch()
        #expect(service.registers == 1)
        #expect(item.state == .on)
        item.applyAtLaunch()
        #expect(service.registers == 1)
    }

    @Test func launchLeavesAnOffSettingAlone() {
        let (defaults, name) = suite()
        defer { defaults.removePersistentDomain(forName: name) }
        defaults.launchAtLogin = false
        let service = FakeService(status: .notRegistered)
        let item = LoginItem(service: service, defaults: defaults)
        item.applyAtLaunch()
        #expect(service.registers == 0)
        #expect(service.unregisters == 0)
        #expect(item.state == .off)
    }

    @Test func turningItOffUnregistersAndOnRegisters() {
        let (defaults, name) = suite()
        defer { defaults.removePersistentDomain(forName: name) }
        let service = FakeService(status: .enabled)
        let item = LoginItem(service: service, defaults: defaults)
        item.setEnabled(false)
        #expect(service.unregisters == 1)
        #expect(!defaults.launchAtLogin)
        #expect(item.state == .off)
        item.setEnabled(true)
        #expect(service.registers == 1)
        #expect(defaults.launchAtLogin)
        #expect(item.state == .on)
        #expect(service.settingsOpened == 0)
    }

    @Test func approvalShowsAndOpensLoginItems() {
        let (defaults, name) = suite()
        defer { defaults.removePersistentDomain(forName: name) }
        let service = FakeService(status: .notRegistered)
        service.statusAfterRegister = .requiresApproval
        let item = LoginItem(service: service, defaults: defaults)
        item.applyAtLaunch()
        // At launch it only shows; System Settings opens when the user turns it on.
        #expect(item.state == .requiresApproval)
        #expect(service.settingsOpened == 0)
        item.setEnabled(true)
        #expect(service.settingsOpened == 1)
        item.openSystemSettings()
        #expect(service.settingsOpened == 2)
    }

    @Test func aFailedRegistrationKeepsTheSetting() {
        let (defaults, name) = suite()
        defer { defaults.removePersistentDomain(forName: name) }
        let service = FakeService(status: .notRegistered)
        service.failsToRegister = true
        let item = LoginItem(service: service, defaults: defaults)
        item.setEnabled(true)
        #expect(service.registers == 1)
        #expect(defaults.launchAtLogin)
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `LoginItem`**

`Stickermine/App/LoginItem.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import Foundation
import ServiceManagement

/// What `LoginItem` needs from `SMAppService.mainApp`, so tests never touch
/// the real login items.
@MainActor
protocol LoginItemService: AnyObject {
    var status: SMAppService.Status { get }
    func register() throws
    func unregister() throws
    /// System Settings › General › Login Items.
    func openSystemSettings()
}

@MainActor
final class LiveLoginItemService: LoginItemService {
    init() {}

    var status: SMAppService.Status { SMAppService.mainApp.status }
    func register() throws { try SMAppService.mainApp.register() }
    func unregister() throws { try SMAppService.mainApp.unregister() }
    func openSystemSettings() { SMAppService.openSystemSettingsLoginItems() }
}

/// Launch at login (spec section 13): `SMAppService.mainApp`, on by default,
/// because stickers only exist while the app runs. The `launchAtLogin`
/// setting is the switch; registering happens when it is on and the service
/// isn't enabled, unregistering when it is turned off. When macOS wants the
/// user's approval first, the setting says so and System Settings opens.
@MainActor
final class LoginItem {
    enum State: Equatable {
        case on
        case off
        /// On, but macOS waits for the user to allow it in Login Items.
        case requiresApproval
    }

    private let service: LoginItemService
    private let defaults: UserDefaults

    init(service: LoginItemService? = nil, defaults: UserDefaults = .standard) {
        self.service = service ?? LiveLoginItemService()
        self.defaults = defaults
    }

    var state: State {
        guard defaults.launchAtLogin else { return .off }
        return service.status == .requiresApproval ? .requiresApproval : .on
    }

    /// Every launch (outside the test host): register when the setting is on
    /// and the service isn't enabled yet. An off setting is left alone.
    func applyAtLaunch() {
        guard defaults.launchAtLogin, service.status != .enabled else { return }
        try? service.register()
    }

    /// The Home toggle. Turning it on registers and, if macOS asks for
    /// approval, opens Login Items; turning it off unregisters.
    func setEnabled(_ on: Bool) {
        defaults.launchAtLogin = on
        if on {
            if service.status != .enabled { try? service.register() }
            if service.status == .requiresApproval { service.openSystemSettings() }
        } else {
            try? service.unregister()
        }
    }

    func openSystemSettings() {
        service.openSystemSettings()
    }
}
```

What changed: every name is Stickermine's (the API URL, the DMG URL and name, both Caskroom paths, `/Applications/Stickermine.app`, `fetch`/`upgrade --cask stickermine`, `stickermine-update.sh`, the releases page); the state is `@Published` for SwiftUI instead of a `refreshUpdateFooter()` call; the request goes through an injectable `fetch` (tests pass canned JSON) and only an HTTP 200 counts as an answer; the helper script is a pure function; and `beforeHelper` (spec section 5.10's cleanup) runs after the script is written and before the helper starts.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the updater**

`Stickermine/Updater/Updater.swift` (complete file):

```swift
import AppKit
import os

/// Where an update is in its lifecycle (Pullcord's `UpdateState`).
enum UpdateState: Equatable {
    /// Running the newest release (or nothing newer carries the DMG yet).
    case upToDate
    /// A newer release with `Stickermine.dmg` exists: Home's strip offers Update.
    case available
    /// Fetching the DMG before opening it and quitting.
    case downloading
    /// The Homebrew helper is upgrading (it quits and relaunches Stickermine).
    case updating
    /// The upgrade didn't finish: the strip offers Update again.
    case failed
}

/// The self-updater (spec section 13), ported from Pullcord's: it asks
/// GitHub for the latest release only where the answer is seen (Home
/// opening, the About version line, and once after a login launch; never
/// polling) and offers a release only if its assets include
/// `Stickermine.dmg`. A Homebrew install (Caskroom entry and running from
/// /Applications/Stickermine.app) upgrades through a detached helper that
/// quits this process by PID, runs `brew upgrade --cask`, clears the
/// quarantine flag and relaunches; any other install downloads the DMG to
/// ~/Downloads (HTTP 200 only), opens it and quits. Before the helper is
/// spawned, the app's termination cleanup runs (`beforeHelper`): the helper's
/// `kill` skips `applicationWillTerminate`.
///
/// Keep Info.plist's version ahead of the newest tag, or every local build
/// offers the release as an update.
@MainActor
final class Updater: ObservableObject {
    nonisolated static let latestReleaseURL = URL(string: "https://api.github.com/repos/grokcodile/stickermine/releases/latest")!
    nonisolated static let dmgName = "Stickermine.dmg"
    nonisolated static let dmgURL = URL(string: "https://github.com/grokcodile/stickermine/releases/latest/download/Stickermine.dmg")!
    nonisolated static let releasesPageURL = URL(string: "https://github.com/grokcodile/stickermine/releases/latest")!
    nonisolated static let caskName = "stickermine"
    nonisolated static let caskroomPaths = ["/opt/homebrew/Caskroom/stickermine", "/usr/local/Caskroom/stickermine"]
    nonisolated static let installedBundlePath = "/Applications/Stickermine.app"
    nonisolated static let brewPaths = ["/opt/homebrew/bin/brew", "/usr/local/bin/brew"]
    nonisolated static let helperScriptName = "stickermine-update.sh"
    /// A successful Homebrew upgrade kills this process; still being here
    /// after this long means it didn't happen.
    nonisolated static let watchdogInterval: TimeInterval = 180

    /// What a `releases/latest` answer says.
    struct Release: Equatable {
        /// The tag without its leading "v".
        let version: String
        /// Its assets include `Stickermine.dmg`.
        let installable: Bool
    }

    @Published private(set) var state: UpdateState = .upToDate
    /// The newest release when it is newer than this build, else nil.
    @Published private(set) var latestVersion: String?

    let appVersion: String
    private let fetch: () async -> Data?
    private let beforeHelper: () -> Void
    private var updateStarting = false
    private var watchdog: Timer?
    private let log = Logger(subsystem: "com.ethan.stickermine", category: "updater")

    /// `fetch` defaults to the GitHub request; tests pass canned JSON.
    init(appVersion: String = Updater.bundleVersion, fetch: (() async -> Data?)? = nil,
         beforeHelper: @escaping () -> Void) {
        self.appVersion = appVersion
        self.fetch = fetch ?? { await Updater.liveFetch() }
        self.beforeHelper = beforeHelper
    }

    /// CFBundleShortVersionString, which CI stamps from the release tag.
    nonisolated static var bundleVersion: String {
        Bundle.main.object(forInfoDictionaryKey: "CFBundleShortVersionString") as? String ?? "0"
    }

    /// Whether a Homebrew cask installed the copy that is running: the
    /// Caskroom entry alone isn't enough, since a dev build run from ./build
    /// would otherwise hand itself to `brew upgrade`, overwrite the
    /// /Applications copy and reopen the dev build.
    nonisolated static func isHomebrewManaged(caskroomExists: Bool, bundlePath: String) -> Bool {
        caskroomExists && bundlePath == installedBundlePath
    }

    var isHomebrewManaged: Bool {
        let caskroom = Self.caskroomPaths.contains { FileManager.default.fileExists(atPath: $0) }
        return Self.isHomebrewManaged(caskroomExists: caskroom, bundlePath: Bundle.main.bundlePath)
    }

    /// Numeric and component-wise, so 1.26 is newer than 1.17, which is newer
    /// than 1.9; a missing component counts as 0 ("1.0" equals "1").
    nonisolated static func isVersion(_ a: String, newerThan b: String) -> Bool {
        let pa = a.split(separator: ".").map { Int($0) ?? 0 }
        let pb = b.split(separator: ".").map { Int($0) ?? 0 }
        for i in 0..<max(pa.count, pb.count) {
            let x = i < pa.count ? pa[i] : 0
            let y = i < pb.count ? pb[i] : 0
            if x != y { return x > y }
        }
        return false
    }

    /// Parses GitHub's `releases/latest` JSON; nil when it has no tag.
    nonisolated static func release(from data: Data) -> Release? {
        guard let json = try? JSONSerialization.jsonObject(with: data) as? [String: Any],
              let tag = json["tag_name"] as? String, !tag.isEmpty
        else { return nil }
        let version = tag.hasPrefix("v") ? String(tag.dropFirst()) : tag
        // CI publishes the release a moment before its DMG upload lands, and
        // both update paths fetch that file: without it, Update would 404.
        let assets = json["assets"] as? [[String: Any]] ?? []
        let installable = assets.contains { $0["name"] as? String == dmgName }
        return Release(version: version, installable: installable)
    }

    /// The only network request Stickermine makes: a GET, nothing sent.
    nonisolated static func liveFetch() async -> Data? {
        var request = URLRequest(url: latestReleaseURL)
        request.setValue("application/vnd.github+json", forHTTPHeaderField: "Accept")
        request.timeoutInterval = 10
        guard let (data, response) = try? await URLSession.shared.data(for: request),
              (response as? HTTPURLResponse)?.statusCode == 200
        else { return nil }
        return data
    }

    /// Asks GitHub now. `completion` (on the main actor) says whether GitHub
    /// answered, so the About line can say "Couldn't check".
    func check(completion: ((Bool) -> Void)? = nil) {
        Task { [weak self] in
            guard let self else { return }
            let data = await self.fetch()
            guard let data, let release = Self.release(from: data) else {
                completion?(false)
                return
            }
            self.handle(release)
            completion?(true)
        }
    }

    /// A check's answer. An update already under way is never interrupted.
    func handle(_ release: Release) {
        guard state != .updating, state != .downloading else { return }
        let newer = release.installable && Self.isVersion(release.version, newerThan: appVersion)
        let newState: UpdateState = newer ? .available : .upToDate
        let newLatest = newer ? release.version : nil
        guard newState != state || newLatest != latestVersion else { return }
        latestVersion = newLatest
        state = newState
        log.notice("latest release \(release.version, privacy: .public) (installable: \(release.installable)); this is \(self.appVersion, privacy: .public)")
    }

    /// The strip's Update button: Homebrew's helper, or the DMG download.
    func performUpdate() {
        if isHomebrewManaged { startHomebrewUpdate() } else { downloadUpdate() }
    }

    // MARK: Homebrew

    /// The detached helper's script. It does the slow part (index refresh and
    /// download) while Stickermine still shows "Updating…", then quits it by
    /// PID (pkill and pgrep skip their own ancestors, and Homebrew won't quit
    /// an app that brew runs inside), upgrades, clears the quarantine flag
    /// (a quarantined app needs an interactive first launch, and the next
    /// login item launch has nobody to approve it) and reopens the app,
    /// unconditionally, so even a failed upgrade brings it back.
    nonisolated static func helperScript(brew: String, bundle: String, pid: Int32) -> String {
        let brewBin = (brew as NSString).deletingLastPathComponent
        return """
        #!/bin/sh
        export PATH="\(brewBin):/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin"
        "\(brew)" update >/dev/null 2>&1
        "\(brew)" fetch --cask \(caskName) >/dev/null 2>&1
        kill \(pid) 2>/dev/null
        for i in $(seq 1 20); do kill -0 \(pid) 2>/dev/null || break; sleep 0.5; done
        kill -0 \(pid) 2>/dev/null && kill -9 \(pid) 2>/dev/null
        "\(brew)" upgrade --cask \(caskName) >/dev/null 2>&1
        xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine "\(bundle)" 2>/dev/null
        open "\(bundle)"

        """
    }

    nonisolated static func brewPath() -> String? {
        brewPaths.first { FileManager.default.isExecutableFile(atPath: $0) }
    }

    private func startHomebrewUpdate() {
        guard !updateStarting, let brew = Self.brewPath() else {
            state = .available
            return
        }
        updateStarting = true
        state = .updating
        let path = NSTemporaryDirectory() + Self.helperScriptName
        let script = Self.helperScript(brew: brew, bundle: Bundle.main.bundlePath,
                                       pid: ProcessInfo.processInfo.processIdentifier)
        do {
            try script.write(toFile: path, atomically: true, encoding: .utf8)
            // Spec section 5.10: screencapture terminated, the library flushed.
            beforeHelper()
            let helper = Process()
            helper.executableURL = URL(fileURLWithPath: "/bin/sh")
            helper.arguments = [path]
            try helper.run()
            log.notice("Homebrew update helper started")
            armWatchdog()
        } catch {
            log.error("the update helper didn't start: \(error.localizedDescription, privacy: .public)")
            updateStarting = false
            state = .failed
        }
    }

    /// No kill within 180 s: brew found nothing to do or the helper died.
    private func armWatchdog() {
        watchdog?.invalidate()
        watchdog = Timer.scheduledTimer(withTimeInterval: Self.watchdogInterval, repeats: false) { [weak self] _ in
            MainActor.assumeIsolated {
                guard let self, self.state == .updating else { return }
                self.updateStarting = false
                self.state = .failed
                // If brew did replace the app after all, this corrects it.
                self.check()
            }
        }
    }

    // MARK: DMG

    /// Downloads the DMG to ~/Downloads, opens it and quits, so the new build
    /// can be dragged over this one. A non-200 answer (GitHub's 404 page)
    /// never replaces an existing ~/Downloads/Stickermine.dmg; the releases
    /// page opens instead.
    private func downloadUpdate() {
        state = .downloading
        let destination = FileManager.default.homeDirectoryForCurrentUser
            .appendingPathComponent("Downloads/\(Self.dmgName)")
        Task { [weak self] in
            do {
                let (temporary, response) = try await URLSession.shared.download(from: Self.dmgURL)
                guard (response as? HTTPURLResponse)?.statusCode == 200 else {
                    try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: temporary)
                    throw URLError(.fileDoesNotExist)
                }
                try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: destination)
                try FileManager.default.moveItem(at: temporary, to: destination)
                NSWorkspace.shared.open(destination)
                // Quit once the mount request is out, so nothing holds the old app.
                DispatchQueue.main.asyncAfter(deadline: .now() + 1.2) {
                    NSApp.terminate(nil)
                }
            } catch {
                let missing = (error as? URLError)?.code == .fileDoesNotExist
                NSWorkspace.shared.open(missing ? Self.releasesPageURL : Self.dmgURL)
                self?.state = .available
            }
        }
    }
}
```
